# DTC P026B

DTC P026B : Injector Performance Problem

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If DTC P026B is indicated alone, do the troubleshooting for DTC P02CC, P02CD, P02CE, P02CF, P02D0, P02D1, P02D2, and P02D3 using the on-board snapshot for P026B.

- If DTC P02CC, P02CD, P02CE, P02CF, P02D0, P02D1, P02D2, P02D3, and P026B are stored at the same time, check for a poor connection or damage at the injector (s).

- If any of the DTCs listed below are indicated at the same time as DTC P026B, troubleshoot those DTCs first, then recheck for P026B. P02CC, P02CD: No. 1 cylinder injector P02CE, P02CF: No. 2 cylinder injector P02D0, P02D1: No. 3 cylinder injector P02D2, P02D3: No. 4 cylinder injector

P02CC, P02CD: No. 1 cylinder injector

P02CE, P02CF: No. 2 cylinder injector

P02D0, P02D1: No. 3 cylinder injector

P02D2, P02D3: No. 4 cylinder injector

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P026B Injector Performance Problem

DTC (PGM-FI)

- DTC check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. YES Go to the indicated DTC's troubleshooting. NO Go to the troubleshooting for DTC P02CC, P02CD, P02CE, P02CF, P02D0, P02D1, P02D2, and P02D3 using the on-board snapshot for DTC P026B.

# DTC P027E

DTC P027E : Cold Start Air Fuel Ratio Control System Command Lean

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P027E Cold Start Air Fuel Ratio Control System Command Lean

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Let the engine cool until the value of ECT SENSOR 1 is 122 deg.F (50 deg.C) or less. Signal Current conditions Values Unit ECT SENSOR 1 -3. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -4. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -5. Start the engine, and let it idle for 10 seconds or more. -6. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P027E Cold Start Air Fuel Ratio Control System Command Lean Is DTC P027E indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting, or substitute a known-good PCM , then recheck. If DTC P027E goes away and the PCM was substituted, replace the original PCM . NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Let the engine cool until the value of ECT SENSOR 1 is 122 deg.F (50 deg.C) or less.

# DTC P02CC, P02CD, P02CE, P02CF, P02D0, P02D1, P02D2, P02D3 (K20C1) (17-21)

DTC P02CC : No. 1 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Minimum Limit

DTC P02CD : No. 1 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Maximum Limit

DTC P02CE : No. 2 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Minimum Limit

DTC P02CF : No. 2 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Maximum Limit

DTC P02D0 : No. 3 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Minimum Limit

DTC P02D1 : No. 3 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Maximum Limit

DTC P02D2 : No. 4 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Minimum Limit

DTC P02D3 : No. 4 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Maximum Limit

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If any of the DTCs listed below are stored at the same time as P02CC through P02D3, troubleshoot those DTCs first, then recheck for P02CC through P02D3. P0201, P0202, P0203, P0204: No. 1-No. 4 cylinder injector (s)

P0201, P0202, P0203, P0204: No. 1-No. 4 cylinder injector (s)

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P02CC No. 1 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Minimum Limit

P02CD No. 1 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Maximum Limit

P02CE No. 2 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Minimum Limit

P02CF No. 2 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Maximum Limit

P02D0 No. 3 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Minimum Limit

P02D1 No. 3 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Maximum Limit

P02D2 No. 4 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Minimum Limit

P02D3 No. 4 Cylinder Injector Offset Learning Exceeds Maximum Limit

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Injector check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Substitute a known-good injector (s) of the problem cylinder (s) . -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -5. Start the engine (If the engine does not start, crank the engine for 20 seconds). -6. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0201 No. 1 Cylinder Injector Circuit Malfunction P0202 No. 2 Cylinder Injector Circuit Malfunction P0203 No. 3 Cylinder Injector Circuit Malfunction P0204 No. 4 Cylinder Injector Circuit Malfunction Is the DTC (s) of the problem cylinder (s) indicated? YES The injector (s) is OK. Go to the indicated DTC's troubleshooting. NO Replace all injectors as a set .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Substitute a known-good injector (s) of the problem cylinder (s) .

-3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-4. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-5. Start the engine (If the engine does not start, crank the engine for 20 seconds).

-6. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0201 No. 1 Cylinder Injector Circuit Malfunction

P0202 No. 2 Cylinder Injector Circuit Malfunction

P0203 No. 3 Cylinder Injector Circuit Malfunction

P0204 No. 4 Cylinder Injector Circuit Malfunction

Is the DTC (s) of the problem cylinder (s) indicated?

YES

The injector (s) is OK. Go to the indicated DTC's troubleshooting.

NO

Replace all injectors as a set .

# DTC P0300, P0301, P0302, P0303, P0304 (K20C1) (17-21)

DTC P0300 : Random Misfire Detected

DTC P0301 : No. 1 Cylinder Misfire Detected

DTC P0302 : No. 2 Cylinder Misfire Detected

DTC P0303 : No. 3 Cylinder Misfire Detected

DTC P0304 : No. 4 Cylinder Misfire Detected

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If the misfire is frequent enough to trigger detection of increased emissions during two consecutive driving cycles, the MIL will come on, and DTC P0300 (and some combination of P0301 through P0304) will be stored.

- If the misfire is frequent enough to damage the catalyst, the MIL will flash whenever the misfire occurs, and DTC P0300 (and some combination of P0301 through P0304) will be stored. When the misfire stops, the MIL will remain on.

- Troubleshoot the following DTCs first, if any of them were stored along with the random misfire DTC (s) (Because parts can sometimes fail without setting DTC's you should also do a physical inspection of the systems listed below): P0087, P0088, P0089: Fuel rail pressure sensor P0101, P0102, P0103: MAF sensor P0106, P0107, P0108: MAP sensor P0171, P0172: Fuel system P0335, P0339: CKP sensor P0340, P0344: CMP sensor A P0351, P0352, P0353, P0354: No. 1-No. 4 cylinder ignition coil (s) P0506, P0507: Idle control system P2623: High pressure fuel pump

P0087, P0088, P0089: Fuel rail pressure sensor

P0101, P0102, P0103: MAF sensor

P0106, P0107, P0108: MAP sensor

P0171, P0172: Fuel system

P0335, P0339: CKP sensor

P0340, P0344: CMP sensor A

P0351, P0352, P0353, P0354: No. 1-No. 4 cylinder ignition coil (s)

P0506, P0507: Idle control system

P2623: High pressure fuel pump

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0300 Random Misfire Detected

P0301 No. 1 Cylinder Misfire Detected

P0302 No. 2 Cylinder Misfire Detected

P0303 No. 3 Cylinder Misfire Detected

P0304 No. 4 Cylinder Misfire Detected

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification - Idle -1. Record the ONBOARD SNAPSHOT with the HDS. -2. Start the engine, and let it idle. -3. Monitor these parameters in the DATA LIST: Signal Current conditions Values Unit Cyl1 Misfire B Cyl2 Misfire B Cyl3 Misfire B Cyl4 Misfire B Are the misfire counts increasing on any cylinder at idle? YES The engine is misfiring at idle, go to step 2. NO The engine does not misfire at idle, go to step 3.

-1. Record the ONBOARD SNAPSHOT with the HDS.

-2. Start the engine, and let it idle.

-3. Monitor these parameters in the DATA LIST:

Signal | Current conditions

Values | Unit

Cyl1 Misfire B

Cyl2 Misfire B

Cyl3 Misfire B

Cyl4 Misfire B

Are the misfire counts increasing on any cylinder at idle?

YES

The engine is misfiring at idle, go to step 2.

NO

The engine does not misfire at idle, go to step 3.

- Determine Affected Cylinders -1. Continue to monitor the misfire parameters in the DATA LIST. Signal Current conditions Values Unit Cyl1 Misfire B Cyl2 Misfire B Cyl3 Misfire B Cyl4 Misfire B Are the misfire counts generally isolated to a single cylinder? YES The misfire is isolated to a single cylinder. If the misfire counts are increasing every engine revolution (completely inactive cylinder), go to Single Cylinder Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 6. If the misfire counts are increasing every few engine revolutions (partially inactive cylinder), go to Single Cylinder Non-Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 20. NO The misfire affects multiple cylinders. If the misfire counts are increasing every engine revolution (completely inactive cylinder), go to Multiple Cylinder Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 29. If the misfire counts are increasing every few engine revolutions (partially inactive cylinder), go to Multiple Cylinder Non-Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 46.

-1. Continue to monitor the misfire parameters in the DATA LIST.

Signal | Current conditions

Values | Unit

Cyl1 Misfire B

Cyl2 Misfire B

Cyl3 Misfire B

Cyl4 Misfire B

Are the misfire counts generally isolated to a single cylinder?

- YES The misfire is isolated to a single cylinder. If the misfire counts are increasing every engine revolution (completely inactive cylinder), go to Single Cylinder Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 6. If the misfire counts are increasing every few engine revolutions (partially inactive cylinder), go to Single Cylinder Non-Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 20.

Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -5. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminals No. 3 (Ignition Control) and body ground. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. YES

NO

Is there about 4 V or more?

YES

There is a short to power on the ignition coil control circuit. Find and repair the failure.

NO

The PCM is malfunctioning. Replace the PCM .

- Check Ignition Coil Control Open Circuit -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. -3. Disconnect the PCM connectors. -4. Check for continuity between the ignition coil control terminal at the PCM connector and at ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 3. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? YES The ignition coil control circuit is not open. Go to step 12. NO Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil and the PCM.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute.

-3. Disconnect the PCM connectors.

-4. Check for continuity between the ignition coil control terminal at the PCM connector and at ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 3.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there continuity?

YES

The ignition coil control circuit is not open. Go to step 12.

NO

Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil and the PCM.

- Check Ignition Coil Control Short to Ground -1. Check for continuity between the ignition coil control terminal at ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 3 and body ground. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? YES Repair the short to ground on the ignition coil control wire. NO The ignition coil control wiring is OK. Substitute a known-good PCM .

-1. Check for continuity between the ignition coil control terminal at ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 3 and body ground.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there continuity?

YES

Repair the short to ground on the ignition coil control wire.

NO

The ignition coil control wiring is OK. Substitute a known-good PCM .

- Check Ignition Coil Power Circuit -1. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 1 (power) and a known-good engine ground. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there battery voltage between the ignition coil power terminal and engine ground? YES The power circuit is OK. Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil (3P connector terminal No. 2) and G101. NO Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil and the ignition coil relay.

-1. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 1 (power) and a known-good engine ground.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there battery voltage between the ignition coil power terminal and engine ground?

YES

The power circuit is OK. Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil (3P connector terminal No. 2) and G101.

NO

Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil and the ignition coil relay.

- Inspect Ignition Coil -1. Inspect the ignition coil and spark plug tube for engine oil contamination. Is the ignition coil covered in engine oil? YES Clean the oil from the ignition coil and spark plug tube. Replace the spark plug tube seals in the cylinder head cover . NO Go to step 15.

YES

Clean the oil from the ignition coil and spark plug tube. Replace the spark plug tube seals in the cylinder head cover .

NO

Go to step 15.

Drive the vehicle under the conditions when the misfire was duplicated. -3. Monitor the misfire parameters in the DATA LIST. Go to step 17. NO There is a failure in the ignition coil or the spark plug. Go to step 16.

YES

NOTE: ECT Sensor 1 must read less than 100 deg.F (38 deg.C) before adjusting the valve clearance. Shortcutting this step may cause incorrect valve clearance and engine misfire. -2. Recheck the MAP SENSOR fluctuation at idle. Does the MAP SENSOR continuously fluctuate 0.04 V or more at idle, no loads, engine hot? YES Adjusting the valve clearance did not correct the excessive MAP sensor fluctuation. Go to step 26. NO The excessive MAP sensor fluctuation was caused by incorrect valve clearance.

- Check Fuel Contamination -1. Remove the fuel tank unit . -2. Inspect for evidence of fuel contamination such as diesel fuel, water, rust, or debris. NOTE: If the misfire occurred shortly after refueling, the likely cause is poor fuel quality. The MIL DIST (MIL distance) value in the DATA LIST, along with the fuel level, can help you make this determination. Incorrect or contaminated fuel typically affects multiple cylinders. But a problem such as water in the fuel can cause a single cylinder misfire that occurs randomly. Is there evidence of fuel contamination? YES Replace the fuel filter and then drain/refill the fuel tank with known-good fuel. NO The fuel system has no signs of fuel contamination. Go to step 53.

- Check Fuel Contamination -1. Remove the fuel tank unit . -2. Inspect for evidence of fuel contamination such as diesel fuel, water, rust, or debris. NOTE: If the misfire occurred shortly after refueling, the likely cause is poor fuel quality. The MIL DIST (MIL distance) value in the DATA LIST, along with the fuel level, can help you make this determination. Incorrect or contaminated fuel typically affects multiple cylinders. But a problem such as water in the fuel can cause a single cylinder misfire that occurs randomly. Is there evidence of fuel contamination? YES Replace the fuel filter and then drain/refill the fuel tank with a known-good fuel. NO The fuel system has no signs of fuel contamination. Go to step 62.

# DTC P0300, P0301, P0302, P0303, P0304 (K20C2)

DTC P0300 : Random Misfire Detected

DTC P0301 : No. 1 Cylinder Misfire Detected

DTC P0302 : No. 2 Cylinder Misfire Detected

DTC P0303 : No. 3 Cylinder Misfire Detected

DTC P0304 : No. 4 Cylinder Misfire Detected

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If the misfire is frequent enough to trigger detection of increased emissions during two consecutive driving cycles, the MIL will come on, and DTC P0300 (and some combination of P0301 through P0304) will be stored.

- If the misfire is frequent enough to damage the catalyst, the MIL will flash whenever the misfire occurs, and DTC P0300 (and some combination of P0301 through P0304) will be stored. When the misfire stops, the MIL will remain on.

- Troubleshoot the following DTCs first, if any of them were stored along with the random misfire DTC (s) (Because parts can sometimes fail without setting DTC's you should also do a physical inspection of the systems listed below): P006A, P0100, P0101: MAF sensor P0107, P0108, P2073, P2074: MAP sensor P0171, P0172: Fuel system P0335, P0339: CKP sensor P0340, P0344: CMP sensor A P0351, P0352, P0353, P0354: No. 1-No. 4 cylinder ignition coil (s) P0506, P0507: Idle control system P2646, P2647, P2648, P2649: VTEC system

P006A, P0100, P0101: MAF sensor

P0107, P0108, P2073, P2074: MAP sensor

P0171, P0172: Fuel system

P0335, P0339: CKP sensor

P0340, P0344: CMP sensor A

P0351, P0352, P0353, P0354: No. 1-No. 4 cylinder ignition coil (s)

P0506, P0507: Idle control system

P2646, P2647, P2648, P2649: VTEC system

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0300 Random Misfire Detected

P0301 No. 1 Cylinder Misfire Detected

P0302 No. 2 Cylinder Misfire Detected

P0303 No. 3 Cylinder Misfire Detected

P0304 No. 4 Cylinder Misfire Detected

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification - Idle -1. Record the ONBOARD SNAPSHOT with the HDS. -2. Start the engine, and let it idle. -3. Monitor these parameters in the DATA LIST: Signal Current conditions Values Unit CYL1 MISFIRE CYL2 MISFIRE CYL3 MISFIRE CYL4 MISFIRE Are the misfire counts increasing on any cylinder at idle? YES The engine is misfiring at idle, go to step 2. NO The engine does not misfire at idle, go to step 3.

-1. Record the ONBOARD SNAPSHOT with the HDS.

-2. Start the engine, and let it idle.

-3. Monitor these parameters in the DATA LIST:

Signal | Current conditions

Values | Unit

CYL1 MISFIRE

CYL2 MISFIRE

CYL3 MISFIRE

CYL4 MISFIRE

Are the misfire counts increasing on any cylinder at idle?

YES

The engine is misfiring at idle, go to step 2.

NO

The engine does not misfire at idle, go to step 3.

- Determine Affected Cylinders -1. Continue to monitor the misfire parameters in the DATA LIST. Signal Current conditions Values Unit CYL1 MISFIRE CYL2 MISFIRE CYL3 MISFIRE CYL4 MISFIRE Are the misfire counts generally isolated to a single cylinder? YES The misfire is isolated to a single cylinder. If the misfire counts are increasing every engine revolution (completely inactive cylinder), go to Single Cylinder Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 6. If the misfire counts are increasing every few engine revolutions (partially inactive cylinder), go to Single Cylinder Non-Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 21. NO The misfire affects multiple cylinders. If the misfire counts are increasing every engine revolution (completely inactive cylinder), go to Multiple Cylinder Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 30. If the misfire counts are increasing every few engine revolutions (partially inactive cylinder), go to Multiple Cylinder Non-Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 48.

-1. Continue to monitor the misfire parameters in the DATA LIST.

Signal | Current conditions

Values | Unit

CYL1 MISFIRE

CYL2 MISFIRE

CYL3 MISFIRE

CYL4 MISFIRE

Are the misfire counts generally isolated to a single cylinder?

- YES The misfire is isolated to a single cylinder. If the misfire counts are increasing every engine revolution (completely inactive cylinder), go to Single Cylinder Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 6. If the misfire counts are increasing every few engine revolutions (partially inactive cylinder), go to Single Cylinder Non-Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 21. NO The misfire affects multiple cylinders.

YES

The ignition coil has a failure. Replace the ignition coil .

NO

There is a problem with the ignition coil circuit. Go to step 8.

- Check Ignition Coil Power and Ground -1. Disconnect the ignition coil 3P connector from the problem cylinder. -2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -3. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminals No. 1 (power) and No. 2 (ground). NOTE: After the vehicle is turned to the OFF (LOCK) mode, this circuit may still show battery voltage for a period of time. This is normal. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there battery voltage? YES The ignition coil power and ground circuits are OK. Go to step 9. NO There is a problem with the ignition coil power or ground circuit. Go to step 13.

-1. Disconnect the ignition coil 3P connector from the problem cylinder.

-2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-3. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminals No. 1 (power) and No. 2 (ground).

NOTE: After the vehicle is turned to the OFF (LOCK) mode, this circuit may still show battery voltage for a period of time. This is normal.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there battery voltage?

YES

The ignition coil power and ground circuits are OK. Go to step 9.

NO

There is a problem with the ignition coil power or ground circuit. Go to step 13.

- Check Ignition Coil Control Short to Power -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminals No. 3 (Ignition Control) and body ground. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there about 4 V or more? YES The ignition coil control circuit is shorted to power. Go to step 10. NO The ignition coil control circuit is not shorted to power. Go to step 11.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminals No. 3 (Ignition Control) and body ground.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there about 4 V or more?

YES

The ignition coil control circuit is shorted to power. Go to step 10.

NO

The ignition coil control circuit is not shorted to power. Go to step 11.

- Isolate Ignition Coil Control Short to Power -1. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. -2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -3. Disconnect the PCM connectors. -4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -5. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminals No. 3 (Ignition Control) and body ground. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there about 4 V or more? YES There is a short to power on the ignition coil control circuit. Find and repair the failure. NO The PCM is malfunctioning. Replace the PCM .

-1. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute.

-2. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-3. Disconnect the PCM connectors.

-4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-5. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminals No. 3 (Ignition Control) and body ground.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there about 4 V or more?

YES

There is a short to power on the ignition coil control circuit. Find and repair the failure.

NO

The PCM is malfunctioning. Replace the PCM .

- Check Ignition Coil Control Open Circuit -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. -3. Disconnect the PCM connectors. -4. Check for continuity between the ignition coil control terminal at the PCM connector and at ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 3. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

- Check Ignition Coil Control Open Circuit -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. -3. Disconnect the PCM connectors. -4. Check for continuity between the ignition coil control terminal at the PCM connector and at ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 3. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute.

-3. Disconnect the PCM connectors.

-4. Check for continuity between the ignition coil control terminal at the PCM connector and at ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 3.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there continuity?

YES

The ignition coil control circuit is not open. Go to step 12.

NO

Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil and the PCM.

- Check Ignition Coil Control Short to Ground -1. Check for continuity between the ignition coil control terminal at ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 3 and body ground. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? YES Repair the short to ground on the ignition coil control wire. NO The ignition coil control wiring is OK. Substitute a known-good PCM .

YES

NO

- Check Ignition Coil Power Circuit -1. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 1 (power) and a known-good engine ground. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there battery voltage between the ignition coil power terminal and engine ground? YES The power circuit is OK. Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil (3P connector terminal No. 2) and G101. NO Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil and the ignition coil relay.

-1. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 1 (power) and a known-good engine ground.

YES

NO

Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil and the ignition coil relay.

- Adjust Valve Clearance -1. Adjust the valve clearances , and inspect the camshaft for excessive wear or grooves. NOTE: ECT Sensor 1 must read less than 100 deg.F (38 deg.C) before adjusting the valve clearance. Shortcutting this step may cause incorrect valve clearance and engine misfire. -2. Recheck the MAP SENSOR fluctuation at idle. Does the MAP SENSOR continuously fluctuate 0.04 V or more at idle, no loads, engine hot? YES Adjusting the valve clearance did not correct the excessive MAP sensor fluctuation. Go to step 27. NO The excessive MAP sensor fluctuation was caused by incorrect valve clearance.

- Adjust Valve Clearance -1. Adjust the valve clearances , and inspect the camshaft for excessive wear or grooves. Recheck the MAP SENSOR fluctuation at idle. Does the MAP SENSOR continuously fluctuate 0.04 V or more at idle, no loads, engine hot? YES Adjusting the valve clearance did not correct the excessive MAP sensor fluctuation. Go to step 51. NO The excessive MAP sensor fluctuation was caused by incorrect valve clearance.

- Check Fuel Contamination -1. Remove the fuel tank unit . -2. Inspect for evidence of fuel contamination such as diesel fuel, water, rust, or debris. NOTE: If the misfire occurred shortly after refueling, the likely cause is poor fuel quality. The MIL DIST (MIL distance) value in the DATA LIST, along with the fuel level, can help you make this determination. Incorrect or contaminated fuel typically affects multiple cylinders. But a problem such as water in the fuel can cause a single cylinder misfire that occurs randomly. Is there evidence of fuel contamination? YES Replace the fuel filter and then drain/refill the fuel tank with known-good fuel. NO The fuel system has no signs of fuel contamination. Go to step 55.

Minimum g/s Maximum g/s Specified RPM Ensure the vehicle meets these conditions: Engine coolant temperature (ECT SENSOR 1) above 176 deg.F (80 deg.C) CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral All electrical loads off Signal Current conditions Values Unit ECT SENSOR 1 -3. Monitor the ENGINE SPEED in the DATA LIST with the HDS. Raise and hold the engine speed steady at the specified rpm. Signal Current conditions Values Unit ENGINE SPEED -4. While holding the rpm steady, check the MAF SENSOR in the DATA LIST. Signal Current conditions Values Unit MAF SENSOR Is the MAF Sensor g/s value within specifications? YES The MAF sensor is reading correctly.

- Check Fuel Contamination -1. Remove the fuel tank unit . -2. Inspect for evidence of fuel contamination such as diesel fuel, water, rust, or debris. NOTE: If the misfire occurred shortly after refueling, the likely cause is poor fuel quality. The MIL DIST (MIL distance) value in the DATA LIST, along with the fuel level, can help you make this determination. Incorrect or contaminated fuel typically affects multiple cylinders. But a problem such as water in the fuel can cause a single cylinder misfire that occurs randomly. Is there evidence of fuel contamination? YES Replace the fuel filter and then drain/refill the fuel tank with a known-good fuel. NO The fuel system has no signs of fuel contamination. Go to step 66.

# DTC P0300, P0301, P0302, P0303, P0304 (L15B7/L15BA/L15BY)

DTC P0300 : Random Misfire Detected

DTC P0301 : No. 1 Cylinder Misfire Detected

DTC P0302 : No. 2 Cylinder Misfire Detected

DTC P0303 : No. 3 Cylinder Misfire Detected

DTC P0304 : No. 4 Cylinder Misfire Detected

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If the misfire is frequent enough to trigger detection of increased emissions during two consecutive driving cycles, the MIL will come on, and DTC P0300 (and some combination of P0301 through P0304) will be stored.

- If the misfire is frequent enough to damage the catalyst, the MIL will flash whenever the misfire occurs, and DTC P0300 (and some combination of P0301 through P0304) will be stored. When the misfire stops, the MIL will remain on.

- Troubleshoot the following DTCs first, if any of them were stored along with the random misfire DTC (s) (Because parts can sometimes fail without setting DTC's you should also do a physical inspection of the systems listed below): P0087, P0088: Fuel rail pressure sensor P006A, P0102, P0103: MAF sensor P0107, P0108: MAP sensor P0171, P0172, P2187: Fuel system P0335, P0339: CKP sensor P0340, P0344: CMP sensor A P0351, P0352, P0353, P0354: No. 1-No. 4 cylinder ignition coil (s) P0506, P0507: Idle control system P2623: High pressure fuel pump

P0087, P0088: Fuel rail pressure sensor

P006A, P0102, P0103: MAF sensor

P0107, P0108: MAP sensor

P0171, P0172, P2187: Fuel system

P0335, P0339: CKP sensor

P0340, P0344: CMP sensor A

P0351, P0352, P0353, P0354: No. 1-No. 4 cylinder ignition coil (s)

P0506, P0507: Idle control system

P2623: High pressure fuel pump

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0300 Random Misfire Detected

P0301 No. 1 Cylinder Misfire Detected

P0302 No. 2 Cylinder Misfire Detected

P0303 No. 3 Cylinder Misfire Detected

P0304 No. 4 Cylinder Misfire Detected

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification - Idle -1. Record the ONBOARD SNAPSHOT with the HDS. -2. Start the engine, and let it idle. -3. Monitor these parameters in the DATA LIST: Signal Current conditions Values Unit CYL1 MISFIRE CYL2 MISFIRE CYL3 MISFIRE CYL4 MISFIRE Are the misfire counts increasing on any cylinder at idle? YES The engine is misfiring at idle, go to step 2. NO The engine does not misfire at idle, go to step 3.

-1. Record the ONBOARD SNAPSHOT with the HDS.

-2. Start the engine, and let it idle.

-3. Monitor these parameters in the DATA LIST:

Signal | Current conditions

Values | Unit

CYL1 MISFIRE

CYL2 MISFIRE

CYL3 MISFIRE

CYL4 MISFIRE

Are the misfire counts increasing on any cylinder at idle?

YES

The engine is misfiring at idle, go to step 2.

NO

The engine does not misfire at idle, go to step 3.

- Determine Affected Cylinders -1. Continue to monitor the misfire parameters in the DATA LIST. Signal Current conditions Values Unit CYL1 MISFIRE CYL2 MISFIRE CYL3 MISFIRE CYL4 MISFIRE Are the misfire counts generally isolated to a single cylinder? YES The misfire is isolated to a single cylinder. If the misfire counts are increasing every engine revolution (completely inactive cylinder), go to Single Cylinder Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 6. If the misfire counts are increasing every few engine revolutions (partially inactive cylinder), go to Single Cylinder Non-Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 21. NO The misfire affects multiple cylinders. If the misfire counts are increasing every engine revolution (completely inactive cylinder), go to Multiple Cylinder Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 30. If the misfire counts are increasing every few engine revolutions (partially inactive cylinder), go to Multiple Cylinder Non-Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 48.

-1. Continue to monitor the misfire parameters in the DATA LIST.

Signal | Current conditions

Values | Unit

CYL1 MISFIRE

CYL2 MISFIRE

CYL3 MISFIRE

CYL4 MISFIRE

Are the misfire counts generally isolated to a single cylinder?

- YES The misfire is isolated to a single cylinder. If the misfire counts are increasing every engine revolution (completely inactive cylinder), go to Single Cylinder Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 6. If the misfire counts are increasing every few engine revolutions (partially inactive cylinder), go to Single Cylinder Non-Continuous Misfire at Idle troubleshooting on step 21. NO The misfire affects multiple cylinders.

Is there about 4 V or more?

YES

There is a short to power on the ignition coil control circuit. Find and repair the failure.

NO

The PCM is malfunctioning. Replace the PCM .

- Check Ignition Coil Control Open Circuit -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Jump the SCS line with the HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. -3. Disconnect the PCM connectors. -4. Check for continuity between the ignition coil control terminal at the PCM connector and at ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 3. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? YES The ignition coil control circuit is not open. Go to step 12. NO Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil and the PCM.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-3. Disconnect the PCM connectors.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there continuity?

YES

The ignition coil control circuit is not open. Go to step 12.

NO

Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil and the PCM.

- Check Ignition Coil Control Short to Ground -1. Check for continuity between the ignition coil control terminal at ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 3 and body ground. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? YES Repair the short to ground on the ignition coil control wire. NO The ignition coil control wiring is OK. Substitute a known-good PCM .

-1. Check for continuity between the ignition coil control terminal at ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 3 and body ground.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there continuity?

YES

Repair the short to ground on the ignition coil control wire.

NO

The ignition coil control wiring is OK. Substitute a known-good PCM .

- Check Ignition Coil Power Circuit -1. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 1 (power) and a known-good engine ground. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there battery voltage between the ignition coil power terminal and engine ground? YES The power circuit is OK. Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil (3P connector terminal No. 2) and G101. NO Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil and the ignition coil relay.

-1. Measure the voltage between ignition coil 3P connector terminal No. 1 (power) and a known-good engine ground.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

YES

The power circuit is OK. Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil (3P connector terminal No. 2) and G101.

NO

Repair the open circuit between the ignition coil and the ignition coil relay.

YES Adjusting the valve clearance did not correct the excessive MAP sensor fluctuation. Go to step 27. NO The excessive MAP sensor fluctuation was caused by incorrect valve clearance.

-2. Turn off all engine and electrical loads (A/C, audio, lights, etc.).

-3. Configure a HIGH-SPEED SNAPSHOT, and select only the MAP SENSOR.

-4. Begin displaying live vehicle data on a line graph.

-5. Configure the MAP SENSOR line graph range for 1.2 V MAX and 0.8 V MIN.

-6. Watch the MAP SENSOR fluctuation. See examples below.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Does the MAP SENSOR continuously fluctuate 0.04 V or more at idle, no loads, engine hot?

YES

The intake manifold pressure fluctuation indicates a mechanical engine failure. Go to step 50.

NO

The intake manifold vacuum fluctuation is OK. Go to step 53.

- Adjust Valve Clearance -1. Adjust the valve clearances , and inspect the camshaft for excessive wear or grooves. NOTE: ECT Sensor 1 must read less than 100 deg.F (38 deg.C) before adjusting the valve clearance. Shortcutting this step may cause incorrect valve clearance and engine misfire. -2. Recheck the MAP SENSOR fluctuation at idle. Does the MAP SENSOR continuously fluctuate 0.04 V or more at idle, no loads, engine hot? YES Adjusting the valve clearance did not correct the excessive MAP sensor fluctuation. Go to step 51. NO The excessive MAP sensor fluctuation was caused by incorrect valve clearance.

Worn or damaged camshaft lobes. NO The engine compression and the cylinder leakage are normal. Go to step 52.

- Check Fuel Contamination -1. Remove the fuel tank unit . -2. Inspect for evidence of fuel contamination such as diesel fuel, water, rust, or debris. NOTE: If the misfire occurred shortly after refueling, the likely cause is poor fuel quality. The MIL DIST (MIL distance) value in the DATA LIST, along with the fuel level, can help you make this determination. Incorrect or contaminated fuel typically affects multiple cylinders. But a problem such as water in the fuel can cause a single cylinder misfire that occurs randomly. Is there evidence of fuel contamination? YES Replace the fuel filter and then drain/refill the fuel tank with known-good fuel. NO The fuel system has no signs of fuel contamination. Go to step 55.

red shortly after refueling, the likely cause is poor fuel quality. The MIL DIST (MIL distance) value in the DATA LIST, along with the fuel level, can help you make this determination. # DTC P0326 (K20C1) (17-21)

DTC P0326 : Knock Sensor Malfunction

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0326 Knock Sensor Malfunction

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Record all the on-board snapshots with the HDS. -2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -3. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -4. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -5. Start the engine. -6. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle. Test-drive the vehicle for several minutes in the range of these recorded on-board snapshot parameters: Engine Speed Vehicle Speed ECT Sensor 1 MAP Sensor (Hi Res) REL TP Sensor On-board Snapshot Signal Current conditions Values Unit Engine Speed Vehicle Speed ECT Sensor 1 MAP Sensor (Hi Res) REL TP Sensor -8. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0326 Knock Sensor Malfunction Is DTC P0326 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the knock sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

-1. Record all the on-board snapshots with the HDS.

-2. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-3. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-4. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-5. Start the engine.

-6. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle.

Test-drive the vehicle for several minutes in the range of these recorded on-board snapshot parameters:

- Engine Speed

- Vehicle Speed

- ECT Sensor 1

- MAP Sensor (Hi Res)

- REL TP Sensor

Signal | Current conditions

Values | Unit

Engine Speed

Vehicle Speed

ECT Sensor 1

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0326 Knock Sensor Malfunction

Is DTC P0326 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the knock sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

- Knock sensor installation check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Check that the knock sensor is installed correctly. Is the knock sensor installed correctly? YES Installation is OK. Replace the knock sensor . NO Installation is not correct, reinstall the knock sensor.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Check that the knock sensor is installed correctly.

YES

Installation is OK. Replace the knock sensor .

NO

````text
# DTC P0326 (K20C2)

DTC P0326 : Knock Sensor Malfunction

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0326 Knock Sensor Malfunction

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Start the engine, and hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle. -5. Hold the engine speed at 1, 500 rpm or more without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) for at least 5 seconds. -6. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0326 Knock Sensor Malfunction Is DTC P0326 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the knock sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-4. Start the engine, and hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle.

-5. Hold the engine speed at 1, 500 rpm or more without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) for at least 5 seconds.

-6. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS.

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -4. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -5. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the knock sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

# DTC P0335 (K20C2)

DTC P0335 : CKP Sensor No Signal

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0335 CKP Sensor No Signal

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine. -4. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0335 CKP Sensor No Signal Is DTC P0335 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the CKP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

### Full Text

````text
# DTC P0339 (K20C1) (17-21)

DTC P0339 : CKP Sensor Circuit Intermittent Interruption

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0339 CKP Sensor Circuit Intermittent Interruption

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification (idling) -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine, and let it idle for 10 seconds. -4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0339 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0339 CKP Sensor Circuit Intermittent Interruption Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, go to step 2. If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine, and let it idle for 10 seconds.

-4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0339 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

DTC Description | OBD STATUS

P0339 CKP Sensor Circuit Intermittent Interruption

Does the HDS indicate FAILED?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3.

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, go to step 2. If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

- Problem verification (test-drive): Test-drive the vehicle for several minutes in the range of these recorded on-board snapshot parameters: ENGINE SPEED VEHICLE SPEED On-board Snapshot -2. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0339 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0339 CKP Sensor Circuit Intermittent Interruption Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the CKP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, retest and check the OBD STATUS again.

Test-drive the vehicle for several minutes in the range of these recorded on-board snapshot parameters:

- ENGINE SPEED

- VEHICLE SPEED

On-board Snapshot

-2. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0339 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

DTC Description | OBD STATUS

P0339 CKP Sensor Circuit Intermittent Interruption

Does the HDS indicate FAILED?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3.

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the CKP sensor and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, retest and check the OBD STATUS again.

- CKP sensor circuit connectors and terminals condition check -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at these locations: CKP sensor PCM Engine ground Body ground Are the connections and terminals OK? YES Go to step 4. NO Repair the connections or terminals.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

Check for poor connections or loose terminals at these locations:

- CKP sensor

- PCM

- Engine ground

- Body ground

Are the connections and terminals OK?

YES

Go to step 4.

NO

Repair the connections or terminals.

- CKP pulse plate visual check -1. Remove the CKP sensor , and check for damage to the CKP pulse plate. Is the pulse plate damaged? YES Replace the CKP pulse plate . NO Replace the CKP sensor .

-1. Remove the CKP sensor , and check for damage to the CKP pulse plate.

Is the pulse plate damaged?

YES

Replace the CKP pulse plate .

NO

Replace the CKP sensor .

# DTC P0340 (K20C2)

DTC P0340 : CMP Sensor A No Signal

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0340 CMP Sensor A No Signal

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine. -4. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0340 CMP Sensor A No Signal Is DTC P0340 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at CMP sensor A and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine.

-4. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0340 CMP Sensor A No Signal

Is DTC P0340 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at CMP sensor A and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

- Determine possible failure area (VCC2 line, others) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. CMP sensor A 3P connector -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle ON mode CMP sensor A 3P connector: disconnected Test point 1 CMP sensor A 3P connector No. 1 Test point 2 Body ground Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there about 5.0 V? YES The VCC2 wire is OK. Go to step 4. NO Go to step 3.

CMP sensor A 3P connector

CMP sensor A 3P connector: disconnected

Test point 1 | CMP sensor A 3P connector No. 1

Test point 2 | Body ground

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

Is there about 5.0 V?

YES

The VCC2 wire is OK. Go to step 4.

NO

Go to step 3.

- Open wire check (VCC2 line) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Jump the SCS line with HDS, and wait more than 1 minute. -3. Disconnect the following connector. PCM connector E (80P) -4. Check the continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode CMP sensor A 3P connector: disconnected PCM connector E (80P): disconnected Test point 1 CMP sensor A 3P connector No. 1 Test point 2 PCM connector E (80P) No. 77 Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? YES The VCC2 wire is OK. Check for any authorized service information related to the DTCs or symptoms you are troubleshooting, or substitute a known-good PCM , then recheck. If DTC P0340 goes away and the PCM was substituted, replace the original PCM . NO Repair an open in the VCC2 wire between the PCM (E77) and CMP sensor A.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode.

-2. Jump the SCS line with HDS, and wait more than 1 minute.

# DTC P0341 (K20C2)

DTC P0341 : CMP Sensor A and CKP Sensor Incorrect Phase Detected

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If the DTC P1009 stored at the same time as DTC P0341, troubleshoot DTC P1009 first, then recheck for DTC P0341.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0341 CMP Sensor A and CKP Sensor Incorrect Phase Detected

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, and allow the engine to cool below 104 deg.F (40 deg.C). -4. Start the engine, and let it idle without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral). -5. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0341 CMP Sensor A and CKP Sensor Incorrect Phase Detected Is DTC P0341 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 5. NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, and allow the engine to cool below 104 deg.F (40 deg.C).

-4. Start the engine, and let it idle without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral).

-5. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0341 CMP Sensor A and CKP Sensor Incorrect Phase Detected

Is DTC P0341 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 5.

NO

Go to step 2.

- VTC system check (VTC TEST with HDS) -1. Select the VTC TEST in the INSPECTION MENU with the HDS, then select the IN-VTC. VTC TEST Is the result OK? YES Go to step 3. NO Go to step 5.

-1. Select the VTC TEST in the INSPECTION MENU with the HDS, then select the IN-VTC.

VTC TEST

Is the result OK?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Go to step 5.

- VTC system check (VTC STATUS check with HDS) -1. Test-drive the vehicle at a steady speed between 19-38 mph (30-60 km/h) for 10 minutes. -2. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit VTC STATUS ON Do the current condition (s) match the threshold? YES Go to step 4. NO Do the test-drive again, then recheck the VTC STATUS.

-1. Test-drive the vehicle at a steady speed between 19-38 mph (30-60 km/h) for 10 minutes.

-2. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

VTC STATUS | ON

Do the current condition (s) match the threshold?

YES

Go to step 4.

NO

Do the test-drive again, then recheck the VTC STATUS.

- VTC system check (OBD status check) -1. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0341 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0341 CMP Sensor A and CKP Sensor Incorrect Phase Detected Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES Go to step 5. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, go to step 3, and recheck.

- VTC oil control solenoid valve A operation check -1. Test VTC oil control solenoid valve A . Is the solenoid valve OK? YES Go to step 6. NO Replace VTC oil control solenoid valve A .

# DTC P0344 (K20C1) (17-21)

DTC P0344 : CMP Sensor A Circuit Intermittent Interruption

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0344 CMP Sensor A Circuit Intermittent Interruption

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification (idling) -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine, and let it idle for 10 seconds. -4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0344 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0344 CMP Sensor A Circuit Intermittent Interruption Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, go to step 2. If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine, and let it idle for 10 seconds.

-4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0344 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

DTC Description | OBD STATUS

P0344 CMP Sensor A Circuit Intermittent Interruption

Does the HDS indicate FAILED?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3.

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, go to step 2. If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

- Problem verification (test-drive): Test-drive the vehicle for several minutes in the range of these recorded on-board snapshot parameters: ENGINE SPEED VEHICLE SPEED On-board Snapshot -2. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0344 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0344 CMP Sensor A Circuit Intermittent Interruption Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at CMP sensor A and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, retest and check the OBD STATUS again.

Test-drive the vehicle for several minutes in the range of these recorded on-board snapshot parameters:

- ENGINE SPEED

- VEHICLE SPEED

On-board Snapshot

# DTC P0365 (K20C2)

DTC P0365 : CMP Sensor B Circuit No Signal

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0365 CMP Sensor B Circuit No Signal

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine. -4. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0365 CMP Sensor B Circuit No Signal Is DTC P0365 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at CMP sensor B and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine.

-4. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0365 CMP Sensor B Circuit No Signal

Is DTC P0365 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at CMP sensor B and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

- Determine possible failure area (VCC2 line, others) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. CMP sensor B 3P connector -3. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -4. Measure the voltage between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle ON mode CMP sensor B 3P connector: disconnected Test point 1 CMP sensor B 3P connector No. 1 Test point 2 Body ground Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there about 5.0 V? YES The VCC2 wire is OK. Go to step 4. NO Go to step 3.

CMP sensor B 3P connector

CMP sensor B 3P connector: disconnected

Test point 1 | CMP sensor B 3P connector No. 1

YES

The VCC2 wire is OK. Go to step 4.

NO

PCM connector E (80P) -4. Check the continuity between test points 1 and 2. If DTC P0365 goes away and the PCM was substituted, replace the original PCM . NO Repair an open in the VCC2 wire between the PCM (E77) and CMP sensor B.

# DTC P0366 (K20C2)

DTC P0366 : CMP Sensor B and CKP Sensor Incorrect Phase Detected

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If the DTC P101A stored at the same time as DTC P0366, troubleshoot DTC P101A first, then recheck for DTC P0366.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0366 CMP Sensor B and CKP Sensor Incorrect Phase Detected

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, and allow the engine to cool below 104 deg.F (40 deg.C). -4. Start the engine, and let it idle without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral). -5. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0366 CMP Sensor B and CKP Sensor Incorrect Phase Detected Is DTC P0366 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 5. NO Go to step 2.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, and allow the engine to cool below 104 deg.F (40 deg.C).

-4. Start the engine, and let it idle without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral).

-5. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0366 CMP Sensor B and CKP Sensor Incorrect Phase Detected

Is DTC P0366 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 5.

NO

Go to step 2.

- VTC system check (VTC TEST with HDS) -1. Select the VTC TEST in the INSPECTION MENU with the HDS, then select the EX-VTC. VTC TEST - EX-VTC Is the result OK? YES Go to step 3. NO Go to step 5.

-1. Select the VTC TEST in the INSPECTION MENU with the HDS, then select the EX-VTC.

VTC TEST - EX-VTC

Is the result OK?

YES

Go to step 3.

NO

Go to step 5.

- VTC system check (VTC STATUS check with HDS) -1. Test-drive the vehicle at a steady speed between 19-38 mph (30-60 km/h) for 10 minutes. -2. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS. Signal Threshold Current conditions Values Unit Values Unit EX VTC Status ON Do the current condition (s) match the threshold? YES Go to step 4. NO Do the test-drive again, then recheck the VTC STATUS.

-1. Test-drive the vehicle at a steady speed between 19-38 mph (30-60 km/h) for 10 minutes.

-2. Check the parameter (s) below with the HDS.

Signal | Threshold | Current conditions

Values | Unit | Values | Unit

EX VTC Status | ON

Do the current condition (s) match the threshold?

YES

Go to step 4.

NO

Do the test-drive again, then recheck the VTC STATUS.

- VTC system check (OBD status check) -1. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0366 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0366 CMP Sensor B and CKP Sensor Incorrect Phase Detected Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES Go to step 5. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, go to step 3, and recheck.

-1. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0366 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

P0366 CMP Sensor B and CKP Sensor Incorrect Phase Detected

YES

Go to step 5.

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, go to step 3, and recheck.

- VTC oil control solenoid valve B operation check -1. Test VTC oil control solenoid valve B . Is the solenoid valve OK? YES Go to step 6. NO Replace VTC oil control solenoid valve B .

Go to step 6.

YES

Go to step 7.

NO

- Cam chain check -1. Check for damage or stretch at the cam chain . Is the cam chain damaged or stretched? YES Replace the cam chain and the auto-tensioner . NO Go to step 8.

-1. Check for damage or stretch at the cam chain .

YES

Replace the cam chain and the auto-tensioner .

NO

Go to step 8.

# DTC P0369 (K20C1) (17-21)

DTC P0369 : CMP Sensor B Circuit Intermittent Interruption

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0369 CMP Sensor B Circuit Intermittent Interruption

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification (idling) -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine, and let it idle for 10 seconds. -4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0369 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0369 CMP Sensor B Circuit Intermittent Interruption Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, go to step 2. If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine, and let it idle for 10 seconds.

-4. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0369 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

P0369 CMP Sensor B Circuit Intermittent Interruption

Does the HDS indicate FAILED?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3.

NO

If the HDS indicates PASSED, go to step 2. If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, keep idling until a result comes on.

- Problem verification (test-drive): Test-drive the vehicle for several minutes in the range of these recorded on-board snapshot parameters: ENGINE SPEED VEHICLE SPEED On-board Snapshot -2. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0369 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0369 CMP Sensor B Circuit Intermittent Interruption Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 3. NO If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at CMP sensor B and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates NOT COMPLETED, retest and check the OBD STATUS again.

# DTC P0420 (K20C1) (17-21)

DTC P0420 : Catalyst System Efficiency Below Threshold

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If some of the DTCs listed below are stored at the same time as DTC P0420, troubleshoot them first, then recheck for DTC P0420. P0137, P0138, P0139: Secondary HO2S (Sensor 2) P0140, P0141: Secondary HO2S (Sensor 2) heater P0300: Random misfire P0301, P0302, P0303, P0304: No. 1-No. 4 cylinder misfire

P0137, P0138, P0139: Secondary HO2S (Sensor 2)

P0140, P0141: Secondary HO2S (Sensor 2) heater

P0300: Random misfire

P0301, P0302, P0303, P0304: No. 1-No. 4 cylinder misfire

- Poor quality fuel may cause this DTC.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0420 Catalyst System Efficiency Below Threshold

DTC (PGM-FI)

- PGM-FI system check 1 -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine. -4. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle. Test-drive the vehicle for 2 minutes or more in the range of these recorded on-board snapshot parameters: Engine Speed Vehicle Speed Baro Sensor REL TP Sensor On-board Snapshot Signal Current conditions Values Unit Engine Speed Vehicle Speed Signal Current conditions Values Unit Baro Sensor REL TP Sensor -6. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0420 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0420 Catalyst System Efficiency Below Threshold Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES Replace the catalytic converter . NO If the HDS indicates EXECUTING, go to step 2. If the HDS indicates OUT OF CONDITION, meet the test conditions and recheck. If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure the system is OK at this time.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine.

-4. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle.

Test-drive the vehicle for 2 minutes or more in the range of these recorded on-board snapshot parameters:

- Engine Speed

- Vehicle Speed

- Baro Sensor

- REL TP Sensor

YES

NO

# DTC P0420 (K20C2)

DTC P0420 : Catalyst System Efficiency Below Threshold

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If some of the DTCs listed below are stored at the same time as DTC P0420, troubleshoot them first, then recheck for DTC P0420. P0137, P0138: Secondary HO2S (Sensor 2) P0141: Secondary HO2S (Sensor 2) heater P0300: Random misfire P0301, P0302, P0303, P0304: No. 1-No. 4 cylinder misfire

P0137, P0138: Secondary HO2S (Sensor 2)

P0141: Secondary HO2S (Sensor 2) heater

P0300: Random misfire

P0301, P0302, P0303, P0304: No. 1-No. 4 cylinder misfire

- Poor quality fuel may cause this DTC.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0420 Catalyst System Efficiency Below Threshold

DTC (PGM-FI)

- PGM-FI system check 1 -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine. -4. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle. Test-drive the vehicle under these conditions: Engine coolant temperature (ECT SENSOR 1) above 158 deg.F (70 deg.C) CVT in D, M/T in 4th or 5th Vehicle speed between 45-75 mph (70-120 km/h) for 5 minutes or more with cruise control set Vehicle speed between 55-75 mph (90-120 km/h) for 10 seconds, then decelerate (with the throttle fully closed). Repeat this three or more times Maintain the vehicle speed at 55 mph (90 km/h) for 4 minutes or more with cruise control set. Signal Current conditions Values Unit ECT SENSOR 1 VEHICLE SPEED -6. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0420 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. DTC Description OBD STATUS P0420 Catalyst System Efficiency Below Threshold Does the HDS indicate FAILED? YES Replace the TWC . NO If the HDS indicates EXECUTING, go to step 2. If the HDS indicates OUT OF CONDITION, meet the test conditions and recheck. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure the system is OK at this time.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine.

-4. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (CVT in P or N, M/T in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle.

Test-drive the vehicle under these conditions:

- Engine coolant temperature (ECT SENSOR 1) above 158 deg.F (70 deg.C)

- CVT in D, M/T in 4th or 5th

- Vehicle speed between 45-75 mph (70-120 km/h) for 5 minutes or more with cruise control set

- Vehicle speed between 55-75 mph (90-120 km/h) for 10 seconds, then decelerate (with the throttle fully closed). Repeat this three or more times

- Maintain the vehicle speed at 55 mph (90 km/h) for 4 minutes or more with cruise control set.

Signal | Current conditions

Values | Unit

ECT SENSOR 1

VEHICLE SPEED

-6. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0420 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS.

P0420 Catalyst System Efficiency Below Threshold

YES

Replace the TWC .

NO

If the HDS indicates EXECUTING, go to step 2. If the HDS indicates OUT OF CONDITION, meet the test conditions and recheck. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the HDS indicates PASSED, intermittent failure the system is OK at this time.

- PGM-FI system check 2 -1. Continue test-drive until a result comes on. -2. Monitor the OBD STATUS for DTC P0420 in the DTCs MENU with the HDS. ### Full Text

````text
# DTC P0441 (K20C2)

DTC P0441 : EVAP System Purge Flow Malfunction

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- If DTC P0441 is indicated alone, do the troubleshooting for DTC P04DF and P04F1 using the on-board snapshot for P0441.

- If DTC P04F1 and P0441 are stored at the same time, check for a poor connection, a blockage, or damage at the EVAP canister purge line between the EVAP canister purge valve and the EVAP canister. Also check for a stuck closed EVAP canister purge valve.

- If any of the DTCs listed below are indicated at the same time as DTC P0441, troubleshoot those DTCs first, then recheck for P0441.

- - P04DF, P04F1: EVAP system purge flow

- - P04DF, P04F1: EVAP system purge flow

P04DF, P04F1: EVAP system purge flow

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0441 EVAP System Purge Flow Malfunction

DTC (PGM-FI)

- DTC check -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P04DF EVAP System High Purge Flow Detected P04F1 EVAP System Low Purge Flow Detected P0441 EVAP System Purge Flow Malfunction Are DTC P04DF or P04F1 and P0441 indicated at the same time? YES Go to the indicated DTC's troubleshooting. NO Go to the troubleshooting for DTC P04DF and DTC P04F1 .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P04DF EVAP System High Purge Flow Detected

P04F1 EVAP System Low Purge Flow Detected

P0441 EVAP System Purge Flow Malfunction

Are DTC P04DF or P04F1 and P0441 indicated at the same time?

YES

Go to the indicated DTC's troubleshooting.

NO

Go to the troubleshooting for DTC P04DF and DTC P04F1 .
````

# DTC P0442, P0455, P0456 (K20C1) (17-21)

DTC P0442 : EVAP System Very Small Leak Detected

DTC P0455 : EVAP System Large Leak Detected

DTC P0456 : EVAP System Very Small Leak Detected

Special Tools Required

Vacuum Pump/Gauge, 0-30 inHg Snap-on YA4000A or equivalent, commercially available

Vacuum/Pressure Gauge, 0-4 inHg 07JAZ-001000B

NOTICE:

The fuel system is designed to allow specified maximum vacuum and pressure conditions. Do not deviate from the vacuum and pressure tests as indicated in these procedures. Excessive pressure/vacuum would damage the EVAP components or cause eventual fuel tank failure.

NOTE:

- Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

- Fresh fuel has a higher volatility that will create greater pressure/vacuum. The best condition for testing is less than a full tank of fresh fuel. If possible, to assist in leak detection, add 1 gallon of fresh fuel to the tank (as long as it will not fill the tank), just before starting these procedures.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0442 EVAP System Very Small Leak Detected

P0455 EVAP System Large Leak Detected

P0456 EVAP System Very Small Leak Detected

Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the FTP sensor, the EVAP canister purge valve, the EVAP canister vent shut valve, and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot . If the vehicle returns with the same DTC, continue with step 4. NO The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

# DTC P0443 (K20C1) (17-21)

DTC P0443 : EVAP Canister Purge Valve Circuit Malfunction

Special Tools Required

Vacuum Pump/Gauge, 0-30 inHg Snap-on YA4000A or equivalent, commercially available

NOTE: Before you troubleshoot, review the general troubleshooting information .

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0443 EVAP Canister Purge Valve Circuit Malfunction

DTC (PGM-FI)

- Problem verification -1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode. -2. Clear the DTC with the HDS. -3. Start the engine. -4. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle. -5. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS. DTC Description Confirmed DTC Pending DTC P0443 EVAP Canister Purge Valve Circuit Malfunction Is DTC P0443 indicated? YES The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2. NO Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the EVAP canister purge valve and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

-1. Turn the vehicle to the ON mode.

-2. Clear the DTC with the HDS.

-3. Start the engine.

-4. Hold the engine speed at 3, 000 rpm without load (in neutral) until the radiator fan comes on, then let it idle.

-5. Check for Pending or Confirmed DTCs with the HDS.

DTC Description | Confirmed DTC | Pending DTC

P0443 EVAP Canister Purge Valve Circuit Malfunction

Is DTC P0443 indicated?

YES

The failure is duplicated. Go to step 2.

NO

Intermittent failure, the system is OK at this time. Check for poor connections or loose terminals at the EVAP canister purge valve and the PCM. If the on-board snapshot of this DTC is recorded, try to reproduce the failure under the same conditions with the on-board snapshot .

- Determine possible failure area (EVAP canister purge valve stuck open failure, EVAP canister purge valve stuck closed failure) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, and allow the engine to cool below 102 deg.F (39 deg.C). -2. Disconnect the vacuum hose (purge line) (A) from the EVAP canister purge valve (B) in the engine compartment, then connect a vacuum pump/gauge, 0-30 inHg, to the EVAP canister purge valve as shown. Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. -3. Start the engine, and let it idle. Is there vacuum? YES The EVAP canister purge valve is stuck open. Go to step 3. NO The EVAP canister purge valve is stuck closed. Go to step 5.

-1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode, and allow the engine to cool below 102 deg.F (39 deg.C).

-2. Disconnect the vacuum hose (purge line) (A) from the EVAP canister purge valve (B) in the engine compartment, then connect a vacuum pump/gauge, 0-30 inHg, to the EVAP canister purge valve as shown.

Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC.

-3. Start the engine, and let it idle.

Is there vacuum?

YES

The EVAP canister purge valve is stuck open. Go to step 3.

NO

The EVAP canister purge valve is stuck closed. Go to step 5.

- Determine possible failure area (EVAP canister purge valve, others) -1. Turn the vehicle to the OFF (LOCK) mode. -2. Disconnect the following connector. EVAP canister purge valve 2P connector -3. Check for continuity between test points 1 and 2. Test condition Vehicle OFF (LOCK) mode EVAP canister purge valve 2P connector: disconnected Test point 1 EVAP canister purge valve 2P connector (female terminals) No. 1: Test point 2 Body ground Courtesy of HONDA, U.S.A., INC. Is there continuity? YES Go to step 4. NO Replace the EVAP canister purge valve .

-2. Disconnect the following connector.

EVAP canister purge valve 2P connector

YES

Go to step 4.

NO
